18.1 sReplay
Route deviation · B-0002

Detour · consent given beforehand

24.8 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Clarke Quay → Bedok Mall, estimate 15.5 km / 21.8 min, pickup 12:14 drop-off 12:46; charged $41.09 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to a human reviewer: the trip is a metered RydeTAXI service (F7) with one off-route segment F3 (12:29–12:39, +3.27 km), and the rider's prior message F9 ("Sure, go ahead.") answering the driver's F8 ("go round via Sims Avenue") is a specific-road agreement whose reach over the driven segment the record cannot tie to Sims Avenue - confirm the driven road for 12:29–12:39, then apply the trace formula 3.27 km × $1.4098/km = $4.61 (distance fare $27.35 ÷ 19.40 km = $1.4098/km) [F4][F3][F7]; no account flag (F17 route:0).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 19.4 km vs optimal route 15.5 km: +3.9 km (+25%).
  • F2 Trip duration 32.2 min vs estimated 21.8 min (+10.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 12:29–12:39: 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km (up to 755 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $5.54 (3.93 km × $1.4098/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $41.09 SGD: base $3.90, distance $27.35 (19.40 km), time $8.26 (31.8 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeTAXI metered service (charged $41.09 with distance $27.35 for 19.40 km and time $8.26 per F7), so it is not a fixed-quote service and detour mileage flows straight into the fare the rider paid.
  • GPS shows one off-route segment, 12:29–12:39, where 6.34 km was driven against 3.07 km on the optimal route, adding 3.27 km (F3); overall the driver drove 19.4 km instead of the optimal 15.5 km (+3.9 km, +25%) per F1, so an unexempted detour exists.
Driver's advocate
  • The only off-route segment in the GPS record is F3 (12:29–12:39, 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, +3.27 km); F1's total +3.9 km is not itself a segment, so under RD-1 only F3 is reviewed, and RD-2's refund is limited to unexempt segments - no segment beyond F3 can be charged against the driver.
  • Before any deviation the driver sent F8 at 12:26 asking 'Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?', and the rider answered F9 at 12:27 'Sure, go ahead.'; the rider's consent timestamp precedes the segment start 12:29 recorded in F3, so the RD-5 'before you go' condition is met and F3 is not an unexempted deviation.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:29:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”; F9: Chat #2 12:27 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Sims Avenue and the rider agreed.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides whether $4.61 is refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Your dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet: nothing has been refunded or charged as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.
Notice to the driver
The dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet, and nothing changes on your account or earnings as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.

Route deviation · B-0013

Two detours · first one agreed

41.3 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to go round the jam near the start, but later he went off the route a second time without asking. I want that part refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Orchard Road, estimate 20.6 km / 28.8 min, pickup 18:31 drop-off 19:18; charged $61.32 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send the file to a human reviewer to fix the scope of the rider's 18:43 "Sure, your call." (RD-5): if it is treated as a general routing authorisation, only F4 is refundable ($6.74); if F3's path cannot be tied to Rifle Range Road and F3 is instead left unexempted, a further $7.98 (5.66 km × $1.4100/km) would also fall due. No account action against the driver (route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 31.0 km vs optimal route 20.6 km: +10.4 km (+50%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.3 min vs estimated 28.8 min (+18.5 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:46–18:56: 7.10 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.43 km, adding 5.66 km (up to 1923 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Off-route segment 19:03–19:12: 5.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.00 km, adding 4.78 km (up to 1680 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $14.64 (10.38 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $61.32 SGD: base $3.90, distance $43.64 (30.95 km), time $12.20 (46.9 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • This is a metered RydeTAXI trip (F8 total $61.32 with a distance component of $43.64 over 30.95 km), so diversion km feed straight into the fare and the RD-2 mileage refund applies.
  • The GPS track records two off-route segments: 18:46–18:56 adding 5.66 km (F3) and 19:03–19:12 adding 4.78 km (F4); together they explain the trip's +10.4 km over the 20.6 km optimal route (F1). Each is a separate deviation to be tested against RD-3/RD-4/RD-5.
Driver's advocate
  • Only two off-route segments exist in this trip, F3 (18:46–18:56, +5.66 km) and F4 (19:03–19:12, +4.78 km); the first is covered by the rider's own consent and only F4 can be chargeable under RD-1/RD-2.
  • The rider gave prior consent to the first deviation in her own message: chat #1 at 18:42 asked 'ok if I go round via Rifle Range Road?' and chat #2 at 18:43 replied 'Sure, your call.' - sent before the F3 segment started at 18:46, per F9, F10, F11.

Route deviation · B-0023

Detour · consent beforehand, driver has history

51.7 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI NEX Serangoon → Suntec City, estimate 11.0 km / 20.7 min, pickup 22:41 drop-off 23:10; charged $32.28 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to human review: decide the RD-5 scope of the rider's Guillemard Road consent against the 22:54–23:03 segment (exempt = nil; counts = $3.00 voucher) and the RD-6 account referral on that basis

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 13.6 km vs optimal route 11.0 km: +2.6 km (+24%).
  • F2 Trip duration 29.8 min vs estimated 20.7 min (+9.1 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 22:54–23:03: 3.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 2.13 km (up to 1095 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.65 (2.59 km × $1.4102/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $32.28 SGD: base $3.90, distance $19.15 (13.58 km), time $7.65 (29.4 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 22:51 driver: “Looks like traffic on Pan Island Expressway is building up, mind if I go round via Guillemard Road?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip is a RydeTAXI metered ride (complaint header: 'RydeTAXI … charged $32.28 (metered)'), so route deviation drives the fare directly and RD-2's RydeTAXI branch applies; the billed distance was 13.58 km against the optimal route of 11.0 km (F7, F1).
  • There is exactly one off-route segment, 22:54–23:03, where the driver covered 3.77 km against 1.64 km on the optimal route, adding 2.13 km and straying up to 1095 m from the optimal route; that segment is an unexempted detour unless an exemption applies (F3).
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip, so the fare follows the meter (base $3.90 + distance $19.15 for 13.58 km + time $7.65 + waiting $0.11 + platform $1.47 = $32.28 per F7); the rider's 'I paid more than the estimate' is therefore measured in metered distance units, not in a fixed quote, and only unexplained extra km can be refunded (RD-2).
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment only: 22:54–23:03, 3.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 2.13 km (F3); overall the trip ran 13.6 km against an 11.0 km optimal route (F1), so the segment in dispute is a single 2.13 km stretch, not the whole 2.6 km excess.

Route deviation · B-0024

Detour · threat language in chat

24.4 s
Dispute statement

The driver went a different way and when I asked he told me to shut up. I was overcharged and felt unsafe.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Marina Bay Sands → Tampines Mall, estimate 15.9 km / 21.4 min, pickup 08:48 drop-off 09:21; charged $44.17 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Hold the automatic distance refund; route the case to a Ryde human reviewer for the SAFE-1 abuse finding on F9 (driver telling the rider to shut up), and include the route-deviation fare computation (5.14 km × $1.4102/km = $7.25 voucher) in the file so the human can execute the refund of $7.25 once the account/conduct review is concluded.

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.40 · account flag → a person

Issue a $2.40 Ryde voucher to the rider for the metered excess-distance on the unexempt off-route segment (voucher form per NS-7), and send the driver's account to Ryde account review + flag_account

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 10.4 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.0 km (+23%).
  • F2 Stationary 4 min 0 s at (1.3149, 103.7641), 14:23–14:27. Not a pickup or drop-off point (3159 m from pickup, 2466 m from drop-off).
  • F3 Trip duration 20.4 min vs estimated 13.8 min (+6.6 min).
  • F4 Off-route segment 14:21–14:31: 3.81 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.11 km, adding 1.70 km (up to 554 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $2.76 (1.96 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $1.15 (4.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $25.33 SGD: base $3.90, distance $14.65 (10.39 km), time $4.16 (16.0 min), waiting $1.15, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment (14:21–14:31) that drove 3.81 km where the optimal route covers 2.11 km, adding 1.70 km and straying up to 554 m from the optimal route, so under RD-1 this is a prima facie unexempted detour segment.
  • Because this is a metered RydeTAXI trip, the excess distance billed of $2.76 (1.96 km × $1.4100/km, taken from this trip's own distance fare ÷ distance km) is the fare consequence of the detour, and it should be refunded as refund_partial for the unexempted segment under RD-2.
Driver's advocate
  • The rider named the stopping point herself: chat #1 at 14:17 says "Can you stop at Clementi Mall? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes." and the driver accepted at 14:17 (chat #2), so the travel to and stop at Clementi Mall is a passenger-designated stop under RD-3(a), not a route deviation the driver imposed.
  • The rider's request was made before the segment began (rider message timed 14:17; the off-route segment runs 14:21–14:31), so the timing requirement of RD-5 for consent preceding the route change is met on the face of the chat, and the consent covers the stop at the location the rider herself named.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F4)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F4] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F4] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 14:21:25 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Chat #1 14:17 rider: “Can you stop at Clementi Mall? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.”; F10: Chat #2 14:17 driver: “Sure”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The rider asked me to stop at Clementi Mall, so I drove there and waited while the rider went in.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. Stops in this segment: F2. (F9, F10, F2)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F4: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $2.40 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.

Notice to the driver

What decided it: you agreed to a stop the rider asked for at Clementi Mall and did stop there, so that part isn't counted as a detour. The extra distance beyond that stop couldn't be verified, so the refund was capped at 1.70 km.

  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 14:19:10 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) but decides the RD-6 flag (the driver has a prior). Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The GPS rerouted me, that's all. Nothing wrong with the fare.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-2 metered service (detour km enters the fare) [case] RydeX is a fixed fare (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so the RD-1 breach is recorded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.

  • The driver never came within 200 m of the pickup at any time - his closest approach was 599 m at 12:06:55 - and the platform never recorded him at the pickup point (driver_arrived_at is null), so NS-1 (i)/(ii) are not satisfied.
Driver's advocate
  • The GPS record shows my client's closest approach to the pickup point was 599 m at 12:06:55, and he stopped 600 m away from 12:05:55 to 12:09:25 - so he was stationary and waiting, not refusing the trip or abandoning the job.
  • My client did tap "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, but the GPS at that moment placed him 600 m from the pickup point and he stayed within 200 m for 0 seconds afterwards; NS-1 (ii) therefore fails on the system record, and the $6.61 cancellation fee cannot stand - it must be refunded in full.

Route deviation · B-0001

Detour · unexplained loop

20.6 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 21 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 24 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Raffles Place → Changi Airport T3, estimate 21.2 km / 28.4 min, pickup 09:07 drop-off 09:43; charged $49.37 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $4.33

Refund $4.33 (Ryde voucher per NS-7) to the rider and record the RD-1 breach on the driver's file; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 24.4 km vs optimal route 21.2 km: +3.2 km (+15%).
  • F2 Trip duration 36.8 min vs estimated 28.4 min (+8.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:24–09:30: 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km (up to 688 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $4.51 (3.20 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $49.37 SGD: base $3.90, distance $34.42 (24.41 km), time $9.58 (36.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”
  • F8 Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The meter billed the rider over the shortest-and-most-direct route: GPS shows 24.4 km actually driven against a 21.2 km optimal route, +3.2 km (+15%), and the fare shows the excess distance was billed at $4.51 (3.20 km × $1.4101/km).
  • One off-route segment (09:24–09:30) added 3.07 km: 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, and the car was up to 688 m off the optimal route; this is a single unexcused detour for the purposes of RD-1.
Driver's advocate
  • There is exactly one off-route segment: 09:24–09:30, where 3.78 km was driven against 0.71 km on the optimal route, adding 3.07 km (F3). Every other metre of the +3.2 km difference (F1) sits in the 200 m route corridor and is map-matching noise, which RD-2 expressly excludes from refund.
  • The rider's own complaint is confined to 'the extra distance' and the meter reading (F1, F6); no published surcharge was added - waiting is $0.00 and the platform fee $1.47 is a published fee (F6), so FARE-1 yields nothing beyond the mileage calculation.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:24:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”; F8: Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $4.33 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Good news: we're refunding $4.33 to you.

Our records show one part of your trip where the driver went off the best route - about 3.07 km further than needed. That extra distance was added to your metered fare, so you paid for it.

We're not refunding the rest of the extra distance, because only that off-route stretch can be refunded under our rules, and it's 3.07 km at your trip's rate.

The $4.33 comes as a Ryde voucher on your account. No action needed from you.

Your driver's file has been noted.

Thanks for flagging it.
Notice to the driver
We've looked at this trip and found one off-route stretch: about 3.07 km driven beyond the best route, which added to the rider's metered fare.

We're not treating it as an approved detour. There was no traffic incident recorded near the route, and the chat doesn't show the rider agreeing to a route or a stop.

So the trip is being refunded $4.33 to the rider as a Ryde voucher, and this is recorded on your file. Your account isn't being flagged, and you can keep driving as normal.

If you think the GPS routed you differently, tell us and we'll look again.

Route deviation · B-0003

Detour · consent only after the fact

30.6 s
Dispute statement

The driver only mentioned the different route after he had already turned off. I said sure because we were already on it, not because I agreed beforehand. I was overcharged for the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Jurong East MRT → NUS, estimate 8.4 km / 13.8 min, pickup 14:21 drop-off 14:40; charged $25.26 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.74

Issue a Ryde voucher of $2.74 to the rider (NS-7) for the unexempted extra mileage on the 14:28–14:34 segment; leave the base fare, $14.95 distance, $4.83 time, $0.11 metered waiting unit and $1.47 platform fee unchanged; record the RD-1 breach on the driver's file.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 10.6 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.2 km (+26%).
  • F2 Trip duration 19.0 min vs estimated 13.8 min (+5.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 14:28–14:34: 3.07 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.12 km, adding 1.94 km (up to 786 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.06 (2.17 km × $1.4104/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $25.26 SGD: base $3.90, distance $14.95 (10.60 km), time $4.83 (18.6 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 14:30 driver: “Jam on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Clementi Road?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was an unmetered-deviation case on a metered RydeTAXI service: actual distance was 10.6 km against an optimal 8.4 km (+2.2 km, +26%), and one off-route segment at 14:28–14:34 drove 3.07 km where the optimal route covers 1.12 km, adding 1.94 km and straying up to 786 m off the optimal route (F1, F3). That segment is an off-route segment under RD-1 and is not exempt unless RD-3/4/5 applies to it.
  • The rider did not consent before the deviation began: the driver's request ("Jam on Ayer Rajah Expressway … can I go round via Clementi Road?") is timestamped 14:30 and the rider's "Sure, no problem." is 14:31, both after the off-route segment had already started at 14:28 (F3, F8, F9). The rider's own account (the complaint) matches this timeline, so the driver's post-trip statement that he "told the rider" before turning off (F11) conflicts with the system timeline and the chat itself and must lose.
Driver's advocate
  • Only one off-route segment is alleged in this trip: 14:28–14:34, 3.07 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.12 km, adding 1.94 km (F3); it is the only candidate deviation to be reviewed under RD-1 and RD-3/RD-4/RD-5.
  • The rider consented in chat to that route change: the driver asked at 14:30 'Jam on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Clementi Road?' (F8) and the rider replied 'Sure, no problem.' at 14:31 (F9), i.e. before the off-route segment ran (14:28–14:34 means the segment had not completed) - the message is a specific named-road authorisation, not a mere general 'fastest route' licence.

  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 14:28:45 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I told the rider I was going via Clementi Road and the rider said sure.” (F11)
  • ? We reviewed your trip and found that part of the route you were taken on was not justified, so you are getting a partial refund of $2.74, issued as a Ryde voucher on your account.

The rest of your fare stays as charged. No flag was placed on your account.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the route on this trip and found that one stretch of it was not justified, so a partial refund of $2.74 is being issued to the rider as a Ryde voucher. The rest of the fare - base, distance, time, waiting and platform fee - stands.

What decided it: the segment where the route left the optimal path was not covered by a rider agreement made before that segment began. A message sent after the detour started does not exempt the distance already driven, and a statement that you told the rider beforehand is not rider consent.

No account flag applies, since you have no upheld route-deviation disputes in the last 90 days.

Route deviation · B-0004

Detour · incident on the replaced stretch

25.5 s
Dispute statement

The driver left the route the app showed and the meter was higher than the estimate. I never agreed to any change.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Punggol Waterway Point, estimate 17.2 km / 23.6 min, pickup 17:28 drop-off 18:01; charged $45.52 (metered)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close the case with no refund; note in the trip record that the 17:38–17:49 deviation was covered by the on-route DataMall accident on the optimal route, and remind the driver to message riders in-app before rerouting (no account action).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 22.2 km vs optimal route 17.2 km: +5.0 km (+30%).
  • F2 Trip duration 33.8 min vs estimated 23.6 min (+10.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 17:38–17:49: 6.56 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km (up to 1911 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $7.19 (5.10 km × $1.4099/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $45.52 SGD: base $3.90, distance $31.37 (22.25 km), time $8.78 (33.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 17:23 rider: “I'm at the main entrance already, in a grey hoodie.”
  • F8 Chat #2 17:24 driver: “On my way, about 4 min, white Honda Vezel.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment on this trip: 17:38–17:49, 6.56 km driven where the optimal route covers only 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km and reaching up to 1911 m from the optimal route (F3); total actual distance was 22.2 km against 17.2 km optimal, +5.0 km / +30% (F1). This is an unexempted off-route segment under RD-1 and triggers the refund computation in RD-2.
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip (complaint: 'the meter was higher than the estimate'; the fare is built from a distance unit of $1.3098/km-equivalent plus time units, F6), so under RD-2 the rider is entitled to a partial refund of the extra distance at this trip's per-km rate: 4.92 km × ($31.37 ÷ 22.25 km) = 4.92 × $1.4099 = $6.94 (rounded to the cent).
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment (17:38–17:49, +4.92 km) is fully exempt under RD-4(a): an LTA DataMall traffic incident - an accident on the Seletar Expressway (towards TPE) after Woodlands Avenue 12 Exit, reported at 17:16 - sat 0 m from the optimal route, i.e. on the replaced stretch, was reported before the deviation began at 17:38, and remained listed during the trip window ending 18:06. No refund for the detour is therefore due under RD-2.
  • The corridor was objectively slow but not free-flowing: the Seletar Expressway on the optimal route recorded speed band 5 (40–49 km/h) and the Tampines Expressway band 4 (30–39 km/h) for 17:17–18:06, corroborating (as RD-4(b) permits, without being relied on alone) that the accident-related congestion was real and not invented after the fact.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✓ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] incident within 300 m of optimal km 7.1–8.7 (F13)
  • – RD-5 consent scope [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)
  • – RD-3 rider-requested stop [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4099/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

What would change this ruling
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We looked into your trip on [date] and your fare stands as charged - no refund is due.

What decided it: your driver's only detour, between 17:38 and 17:49, was covered by an accident on the Seletar Expressway reported shortly before and sitting right on the route you were taking. When a recorded traffic incident blocks the usual route, the extra distance isn't refundable.

The fare you paid was correct in every part, so there's nothing to add or take back.

Nothing changes on your account.
Notice to the driver
Your trip has been reviewed and closed with no refund and no action against your account.

What decided it: the 17:38–17:49 detour was covered by a recorded accident on the Seletar Expressway reported before the detour and located on the route you were driving, so the extra distance isn't refundable. Separately, you have no upheld route disputes in the last 90 days.

One reminder: message the rider in the app before you reroute, so they know what's happening. That's a note on the trip only - nothing is being taken off your account.

  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:13:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Ayer Rajah Expressway was crawling and flooding in the heavy rain, so I went round it. Any driver would have done the same.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $7.06 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “There was an accident on Kallang Paya Lebar Expressway ahead, lanes were blocked, so I went round it. The extra distance was unavoidable.” (F9)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F4: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $4.41 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your trip and found the route taken included one detour that was not justified. Trips of this kind are metered, so those extra kilometres fed directly into your fare. We are refunding the extra distance at $4.41, issued as a Ryde voucher to your account. The other detour on your trip was covered by an accident reported on the road you would otherwise have taken, so no refund applies to that part. Nothing changes on your account as a result of this review.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed a route dispute on this trip. One deviation was covered by a reported accident on the affected road, so that part is fine. The second deviation, later in the trip, had no qualifying incident and no rider agreement on record, so it is recorded on your file as an unexcused route deviation. A $4.41 voucher has been issued to the rider for the extra distance. No account flag applies: this is a single recorded deviation, not a repeated pattern.   • The trip is a RydeTAXI metered trip (fare breakdown in F6 shows meter components base $3.90 + distance $15.14 for 10.74 km + time $4.29), so the fare follows the taxi company meter and any route dispute is measured under RD-2's RydeTAXI branch, not by comparing the quoted 8.4 km estimate with the meter.
  • Only one off-route segment is recorded: 12:12–12:16, 2.93 km driven against 0.80 km on the optimal route, adding 2.13 km (F3); the total trip excess of +2.3 km over 8.4 km (F1) is therefore almost entirely that single segment, and the residual ~0.2 km is map-matching noise outside any segment (F1) which RD-2 leaves unrefunded.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:12:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4097/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)

Route deviation · B-0049

Detour · pickup chat contains a threat hint word (ordinary message)

21.9 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 11 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 13 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Orchard Road → NUS, estimate 10.7 km / 17.3 min, pickup 09:43 drop-off 10:03; charged $29.11 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.74

Refund $2.74 to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) for the unexempted off-route segment; record the RD-1 breach on the driver's file. No account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 13.1 km vs optimal route 10.7 km: +2.4 km (+23%).
  • F2 Trip duration 20.2 min vs estimated 17.3 min (+2.9 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:47–09:53: 3.83 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km (up to 777 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.44 (2.44 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $29.11 SGD: base $3.90, distance $18.50 (13.12 km), time $5.24 (20.2 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”
  • F8 Chat record: 1 message(s), every one timestamped.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment on this RydeTAXI trip: 09:47–09:53, 3.83 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km and straying up to 777 m from the optimal route (F3); overall the trip ran 13.1 km against a 10.7 km optimal route, +2.4 km / +23% (F1).
  • No exemption applies to that segment: no traffic incident was recorded near the route during the trip window (F12), the only speed-band records are band 4 (30–39 km/h) on Ayer Rajah Expressway and Kent Ridge Road during 09:29–10:08 (F10, F11), and band 4 is nowhere near the band 1–2 congestion level, while weather was fair with 0.0 mm rain (F13) - so RD-4 gives the driver no reasonable cause.
Driver's advocate
  • The excess billed on this trip is attributable to a single off-route segment: 3.83 km driven 09:47–09:53 where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, i.e. 1.94 km extra, out of a total +2.4 km (F3, F1). Everything the rider can recover is confined to that one segment, because RD-2 refunds only the per-km mileage of unexempted segments and expressly leaves the residual gap between the fare's excess distance and the segment sum unrefunded.
  • No traffic justification exists for that segment, and I do not pretend otherwise: no incident was recorded near the route in the trip window (F12), and the two speed-band records - Ayer Rajah Expressway band 4, 30–39 km/h, and Kent Ridge Road band 4, 30–39 km/h (F10, F11) - are neither incidents under RD-4(a) nor band 1/2, so they do not exempt the segment under RD-4(b).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:47:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”; F9: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I followed my GPS; it rerouted me. Nothing wrong with the fare.” (F7, F9)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. hint word in F7 - a locator, not a finding; read it in context (F7)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $2.74 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
You'll get a $2.74 Ryde voucher for part of your fare.

Your trip was metered, so detour distance added to the price you paid. We checked the GPS record: between 09:47 and 09:53 the car drove 3.83 km where the shortest route is 1.89 km, up to 777 m off course, with no traffic incident, no bad weather and no message from you agreeing to that route.

Because only part of the extra distance is refundable under our rules, the voucher covers 1.94 km at your trip's per-km rate. It's a voucher, not a card refund. No flag was placed on your account.
Notice to the driver
A $2.74 voucher is being issued to the rider for this trip, and a breach note goes on your record.

The GPS record shows one off-route stretch: 3.83 km driven where the shortest route is 1.89 km, up to 777 m off course, with no traffic incident, no bad weather and no rider route instruction or agreement on file. Your explanation that your GPS rerouted you doesn't excuse it, and there's no evidence the rider consented to that route.

Nothing else changes on your account - no flag, and the amount recovered is limited to the extra distance we could attribute to that stretch.
